This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of the recalled products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent further sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep a record of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the date and time of the recall, the products removed from sale, and any communication with the supplier or manufacturer. 2026-11-16 13:26:05,822 YOU RECEIVE topic=s1339_final correlation preserved=True {"correlation_id": "edffb9ec-cf32-45c1-a675-f5c918635d3d", "status": "approved", "answer": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

2026-11-15 15:54:53,104 WRITER SEND topic=s1339_drafts {"correlation_id": "4ea1945e-1207-49ce-a6b5-344988077b6c", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

2026-11-16 13:26:05,730 WRITER SEND topic=s1339_drafts {"correlation_id": "edffb9ec-cf32-45c1-a675-f5c918635d3d", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. 2026-11-15 15:56:28,085 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=126 raw={"correlation_id": "4ea1945e-1207-49ce-a6b5-344988077b6c", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

This can be done by checking the product's packaging, labeling, or documentation.\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately isolate the affected product from sale and inventory by:\n\t* Removing the product from shelves and display areas.\n\t* Blocking sales of the product to prevent further distribution.\n\t* Notifying the supplier or manufacturer to confirm the affected lot.\n3. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the following parties:\n\t* Store management and quality control team.\n\t* Supplier or manufacturer (if not already notified).\n\t* Regulatory authorities (if required by law).\n4. **Document actions**: Keep a record of the actions taken, including:\n\t* The date and time the product was isolated.\n\t* The reason for the isolation (i.e., unreadable lot code).\n\t* The steps taken to notify the supplier or manufacturer.\n\t* Any further actions taken, such as returning or disposing of the product.\n\nIt is essential to follow the specific recall notice and supplier instructions for handling the affected product, as they may provide additional guidance on procedures to ensure compliance with regulations and minimize potential risks."}

take the following steps:\n\n**Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.**\n\n* Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and affected lot(s) that need to be isolated.\n* Verify the product's UPC or other identifying codes to ensure accuracy.\n\n**Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.**\n\n* Isolate the affected product by removing it from the sales floor and storing it in a secure, designated area.\n* Block sales of the product by:\n\t+ Temporarily removing the product from the point-of-sale system's inventory.\n\t+ Using a temporary or placeholder product in the point-of-sale system to prevent sales of the recalled product.\n\t+ Informing cashiers and other sales staff not to ring up sales of the affected product.\n* Consider using a \"hold\" or \"block\" code in the point-of-sale system to prevent sales of the recalled product.\n\n**Describe notification, disposition, and documentation.**\n\n* Notify the supplier and relevant authorities (e.g., FDA, CPSC) of the recall and the store's actions.\n* Document the store's actions, including:\n\t+ The date and time the recall was notified to the supplier and authorities.\n\t+ The steps taken to isolate the affected product and block sales.\n\t+ The date and time the product was removed from sale and the reason for the removal.\n\t+ Any other relevant details or documentation required by the recall notice or supplier instructions.\n\nBy following these steps, a store can prevent checkout sales of a recalled product and ensure compliance with the recall notice and supplier instructions."}

2026-11-15 17:02:35,409 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=145 raw={"correlation_id": "f1bf7604-37a3-466b-8ed7-aaf97b24a717", "question": "What should receiving staff do if a supplier delivers another shipment of the affected lot after the recall begins?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "If a supplier delivers another shipment of the affected lot after the recall begins, receiving staff should:\n\n1. **Verify the shipment**: Check the shipment documentation to confirm that it is indeed the affected lot and not a replacement or new product.\n2. **Notify the receiving team lead or supervisor**: Inform the receiving team lead or supervisor immediately, so they can escalate the issue to the relevant parties.\n3. **Isolate the new shipment**: Immediately isolate the new shipment from the rest of the inventory to prevent further distribution or sale.\n4. **Block sales**: Prevent any sales of the new shipment until further notice from the supplier or regulatory authorities.\n5. **Notify the supplier**: Inform the supplier that the shipment is being held and that further instructions will be provided.\n6. **Document the incident**: Record the details of the incident, including the shipment number, date, and time, in the company's recall log or incident report.\n\nThe receiving staff should follow the supplier's instructions for holding, returning, or disposing of the affected product, as outlined in the recall notice."}

2026-11-15 17:03:51,688 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=147 raw={"correlation_id": "c11dc189-2893-4888-a6b4-3ec51bde1370", "question": "How should a store reconcile the quantities received, sold, isolated, and returned for a recalled lot?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "To reconcile the quantities received, sold, isolated, and returned for a recalled lot, follow these steps:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) affected.\n2. **Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking**: \n   - Isolate the affected product by removing it from shelves, displays, and sales floors.\n   - Block sales of the affected product by updating inventory management systems, such as point-of-sale (POS) systems and enterprise resource planning (ERP) systems, to prevent further sales.\n3. **Describe notification, disposition, and documentation**:\n   - Notify the supplier and relevant authorities (e.g., FDA, state health departments) of the recall and provide documentation of the actions taken.\n   - Dispose of the affected product in accordance with the supplier's instructions and local regulations. This may involve returning the product to the supplier or a designated facility for destruction.\n   - Document all actions taken, including:\n     - The date and time of the recall notice\n     - The steps taken to isolate and block sales of the affected product\n     - The notification sent to the supplier and relevant authorities\n     - The disposition of the affected product\n     - Any inventory adjustments or returns made\n\nBy following these steps, you can ensure that the recalled product is properly handled and that the store's inventory is accurately reconciled."}

2026-11-15 17:09:58,932 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=153 raw={"correlation_id": "49c52bcf-c60e-4569-8d21-1bc511f9107d", "question": "What should a grocery store do if two supplier notices give conflicting instructions for the same recalled lot?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "If a grocery store receives two supplier notices with conflicting instructions for the same recalled lot, the store should follow these steps:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Verify the product and affected lot number from both recall notices to ensure accuracy.\n2. **Contact the suppliers**: Reach out to both suppliers to clarify the conflicting instructions. Ask each supplier to confirm their instructions and provide a clear explanation for the discrepancy.\n3. **Determine the correct course of action**: Based on the clarification, decide which instruction to follow. If the instructions are still unclear, consider contacting the supplier's customer service or regulatory affairs department for further guidance.\n4. **Inventory isolation and sale blocking**: Implement the agreed-upon instruction to isolate the affected inventory and block sales of the product. This may involve:\n\t* Removing the product from shelves and display areas.\n\t* Notifying the store's management and staff about the recall.\n\t* Blocking sales of the product through the point-of-sale system.\n5. **Notification, disposition, and documentation**: Follow the agreed-upon instruction for notification, disposition, and documentation. This may include:\n\t* Notifying customers who have purchased the affected product.\n\t* Returning or disposing of the affected product in accordance with the supplier's instructions.\n\t* Documenting the recall, including the product, affected lot, and actions taken.\n\nIt is essential to follow the supplier's instructions and take necessary steps to ensure the safe removal of the recalled product from the store's inventory."}

Verify the product's serial number or batch code to determine if it's part of the recalled batch.\n2. **Inventory isolation and sale blocking**: Immediately stop selling the recalled product and isolate it from other inventory. This will prevent further sales and minimize potential exposure to customers. Block sales of the product in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n3. **Notification, disposition, and documentation**: Notify the customer that the product is part of a recall and inform them of the necessary steps to return it. Provide the customer with information on how to return the product, such as contacting the supplier or a designated return location.\n   - Document the customer's return, including the product's serial number or batch code, the reason for the return, and the date of the return.\n   - Keep a record of the customer's contact information and any communication regarding the return.\n   - If the supplier provides a return shipping label, use it to ship the product back to the supplier.\n   - Once the product is received by the supplier, confirm with the supplier that it has been disposed of in accordance with their procedures.\n\n**Final Answer**: Handle the customer return by isolating the product, blocking sales, notifying the customer, and documenting the return, while following the supplier's instructions for return and disposal of the recalled product."}

Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice. This involves reviewing the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot numbers that are affected.\n2. Check inventory records and transfer documents to verify that the affected products were transferred to the other branch before the recall. This may involve reviewing inventory management systems, transfer logs, and other records to ensure that the products were transferred in a timely manner.\n3. Isolate the affected products by removing them from sale and inventory. This may involve blocking sales of the product at the affected branch and isolating the product in a secure area to prevent further sale or distribution.\n4. Notify the supplier and other relevant parties of the affected products. This may involve contacting the supplier to report the affected products and requesting guidance on how to proceed with the recall.\n5. Document all actions taken in response to the recall, including the identification of affected products, isolation of inventory, blocking of sales, notification of the supplier, and any other relevant actions.\n\nFinal answer: The store should check inventory records and transfer documents to verify that the affected products were transferred to the other branch before the recall, and then isolate the products, notify the supplier, and document all actions taken."}

2026-11-15 17:38:07,746 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=165 raw={"correlation_id": "69a597da-a656-4d73-ae46-d17ed2924ef7", "question": "What records should a store keep when a supplier collects recalled stock from the hold area?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "When a supplier collects recalled stock from the hold area, a store should keep the following records:\n\n1. **Product and Affected Lot**: A record of the product and affected lot number from the recall notice, which will be used to identify the specific products being returned.\n2. **Inventory Isolation and Sale Blocking**: A record of the steps taken to isolate the affected products from sale, including:\n\t* Date and time the products were removed from sale\n\t* Description of the isolation process (e.g., tagging, blocking, or removing from shelves)\n3. **Notification, Disposition, and Documentation**: A record of the following:\n\t* Date and time the supplier arrived to collect the recalled products\n\t* Description of the products being returned (including product name, lot number, and quantity)\n\t* Signature of the supplier representative or store personnel\n\t* Witness signature (if applicable)\n\t* Date and time the products were received by the supplier\n\t* A copy of the recall notice and any relevant documentation (e.g., inspection reports, packaging changes)\n\nThese records will help the store demonstrate compliance with the recall notice and supplier instructions, and provide a clear audit trail in case of any future issues or investigations."}

2026-11-15 17:40:05,876 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=167 raw={"correlation_id": "8fbdcadd-2c3d-4ce3-bb2b-165bb6b5ad6a", "question": "How should a store follow up when its stock system shows affected units on hand but staff cannot locate them?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "To follow up on affected units that cannot be located, a store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to confirm the product and affected lot number. This information will be crucial in determining the next steps.\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately isolate the affected product from the rest of the inventory to prevent further sales and potential exposure to customers. This can be done by:\n\t* Moving the product to a separate, secure area of the store.\n\t* Using tape or other visual indicators to clearly mark the product as \"Recalled\" or \"Isolated\".\n\t* Informing staff not to handle or sell the product.\n3. **Block sales**: Prevent sales of the affected product by:\n\t* Notifying the point-of-sale system to block sales of the product.\n\t* Informing cashiers and sales staff not to ring up sales of the product.\n\t* Displaying clear signage indicating that the product is recalled and not for sale.\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the following parties:\n\t* Store management and district managers.\n\t* Supplier or manufacturer's customer service department.\n\t* Any other relevant stakeholders, such as quality control or logistics teams.\n5. **Document actions**: Keep a record of the following:\n\t* The steps taken to isolate and block sales of the affected product.\n\t* The date and time the recall was initiated.\n\t* Any communication with the supplier or manufacturer.\n\t* The actions taken to dispose of the affected product (if necessary).\n\nBy following these steps, the store can ensure that the affected product is properly isolated, sales are blocked, and responsible parties are notified, while also maintaining a record of the actions taken."}

2026-11-15 17:40:05,916 REVIEWER SEND topic=s1339_final {"correlation_id": "8fbdcadd-2c3d-4ce3-bb2b-165bb6b5ad6a", "status": "approved", "answer": "To follow up on affected units that cannot be located, a store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to confirm the product and affected lot number. This information will be crucial in determining the next steps.\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately isolate the affected product from the rest of the inventory to prevent further sales and potential exposure to customers. This can be done by:\n\t* Moving the product to a separate, secure area of the store.\n\t* Using tape or other visual indicators to clearly mark the product as \"Recalled\" or \"Isolated\".\n\t* Informing staff not to handle or sell the product.\n3. **Block sales**: Prevent sales of the affected product by:\n\t* Notifying the point-of-sale system to block sales of the product.\n\t* Informing cashiers and sales staff not to ring up sales of the product.\n\t* Displaying clear signage indicating that the product is recalled and not for sale.\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the following parties:\n\t* Store management and district managers.\n\t* Supplier or manufacturer's customer service department.\n\t* Any other relevant stakeholders, such as quality control or logistics teams.\n5. **Document actions**: Keep a record of the following:\n\t* The steps taken to isolate and block sales of the affected product.\n\t* The date and time the recall was initiated.\n\t* Any communication with the supplier or manufacturer.\n\t* The actions taken to dispose of the affected product (if necessary).\n\nBy following these steps, the store can ensure that the affected product is properly isolated, sales are blocked, and responsible parties are notified, while also maintaining a record of the actions taken."}

2026-11-16 13:19:31,912 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=173 raw={"correlation_id": "58f5f6dd-f86e-4d7f-bc11-faadae75d657", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

2026-11-16 14:00:45,719 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=183 raw={"correlation_id": "c0bfc018-fc06-4fa7-a45e-0a39a6cdced7", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of the recalled products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent further sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep a record of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the date and time of the recall, the products removed from sale, and any communication with the supplier or manufacturer. This documentation will be important for tracking the store's compliance with the recall notice and for any future investigations or audits."}

2026-11-16 14:03:28,276 REVIEWER RECEIVE topic=s1339_drafts partition=0 offset=184 raw={"correlation_id": "4f8df477-2dfe-4c72-8069-aa47bb21e1f3", "question": "What should a grocery store do after receiving a supplier recall notice?", "plan": ["Identify the product and affected lot from the recall notice.", "Describe inventory isolation and sale blocking.", "Describe notification, disposition, and documentation."], "draft": "After receiving a supplier recall notice, a grocery store should:\n\n1. **Identify the product and affected lot**: Review the recall notice to determine the specific product and lot number(s) that are being recalled. This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}

This information will help the store to isolate and manage the affected inventory.\n\n2. **Isolate inventory**: Immediately remove all products from the shelves and storage areas that are part of the affected lot. This will prevent further sale of potentially contaminated products to customers. The store should also isolate the affected products from other inventory to prevent cross-contamination.\n\n3. **Block sales**: Post clear signs on the shelves and in the store's point-of-sale systems to alert customers that the products are being recalled. The store should also block sales of the affected products in the store's inventory management system to prevent accidental sales.\n\n4. **Notify responsible people**: Inform the supplier, manufacturer, and any other relevant parties of the recall notice. The store should also notify its own management and quality control teams to ensure that the necessary actions are taken to address the recall.\n\n5. **Document actions**: Keep detailed records of the actions taken in response to the recall notice, including the identification of the affected products, isolation and blocking of sales, notification of responsible parties, and any other relevant steps taken. This documentation will be important for compliance with regulatory requirements and for demonstrating the store's efforts to address the recall."}
